\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep/releases\"\u003eevery\nrelease\u003c/a\u003e. ripgrep is similar to\nother popular search tools like The Silver Searcher, ack and grep.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep/actions\"\u003e\u003cimg src=\"https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep/workflows/ci/badge.svg\" performance and smooth\nout the differences between tools (because performance is dominated by how\nquickly one can handle a match and not the algorithm used to detect the match,\ngenerally class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhy should I use ripgrep?\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-why-should-i-use-ripgrep\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Why should I use ripgrep?\" href=\"#why-should-i-use-ripgrep\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eIt can replace many use cases served by other search tools\nbecause it contains most of their features and is generally faster. (See\n\u003ca href=\"/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/FAQ.md#posix4ever\"\u003ethe FAQ\u003c/a\u003e for more details on whether ripgrep can truly\nreplace grep.)\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eLike other tools specialized to code search, ripgrep defaults to\n\u003ca href=\"/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#recursive-search\"\u003erecursive search\u003c/a\u003e and does \u003ca href=\"/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#automatic-filtering\"\u003eautomatic\nfiltering\u003c/a\u003e. Namely, ripgrep won't search files\nignored by your \u003ccode\u003e.gitignore\u003c/code\u003e/\u003ccode\u003e.ignore\u003c/code\u003e/\u003ccode\u003e.rgignore\u003c/code\u003e files, it won't search\nhidden files and it won't search binary files. Automatic filtering can be\ndisabled with \u003ccode\u003erg -uuu\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eripgrep can \u003ca href=\"/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#manual-filtering-file-types\"\u003esearch specific types of files\u003c/a\u003e.\nFor example, \u003ccode\u003erg -tpy foo\u003c/code\u003e limits your search to Python files and \u003ccode\u003erg -Tjs foo\u003c/code\u003e excludes JavaScript files from your search. ripgrep can be taught about\nnew file types with custom matching rules.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eripgrep supports many features found in \u003ccode\u003egrep\u003c/code\u003e, such as showing the context\nof search results, searching multiple patterns, highlighting matches with\ncolor and full Unicode support. Unlike GNU grep, ripgrep stays fast while\nsupporting Unicode (which is always on).\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eripgrep has optional support for switching its regex engine to use PCRE2.\nAmong other things, this makes it possible to use look-around and\nbackreferences in your patterns, which are not supported in ripgrep's default\nregex engine. PCRE2 support can be enabled with \u003ccode\u003e-P/--pcre2\u003c/code\u003e (use PCRE2\nalways) or \u003ccode\u003e--auto-hybrid-regex\u003c/code\u003e (use PCRE2 only if needed). An alternative\nsyntax is provided via the \u003ccode\u003e--engine (default|pcre2|auto)\u003c/code\u003e option.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eripgrep has \u003ca href=\"/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#replacements\"\u003erudimentary support for replacements\u003c/a\u003e,\nwhich permit rewriting output based on what was matched.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eripgrep supports \u003ca href=\"/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#file-encoding\"\u003esearching files in text encodings\u003c/a\u003e\nother than UTF-8, such as UTF-16, latin-1, GBK, EUC-JP, Shift_JIS and more.\n(Some support for automatically detecting UTF-16 is provided. Other text\nencodings must be specifically specified with the \u003ccode\u003e-E/--encoding\u003c/code\u003e flag.)\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eripgrep supports searching files compressed in a common format (brotli,\nbzip2, gzip, lz4, lzma, xz, or zstandard) with the \u003ccode\u003e-z/--search-zip\u003c/code\u003e flag.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eripgrep supports\n\u003ca href=\"/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#preprocessor\"\u003earbitrary input preprocessing filters\u003c/a\u003e\nwhich could be PDF text extraction, less supported decompression, decrypting,\nautomatic encoding detection and so on.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eripgrep can be configured via a\n\u003ca href=\"/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#configuration-file\"\u003econfiguration file\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIn other words, use ripgrep if you like speed, filtering by default, fewer\nbugs and Unicode support.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eWhy shouldn't I use ripgrep?\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-why-shouldnt-i-use-ripgrep\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Why shouldn't I use ripgrep?\" href=\"#why-shouldnt-i-use-ripgrep\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eDespite initially not wanting to add every feature under the sun to ripgrep,\nover time, ripgrep has grown support for most features found in other file\nsearching tools. This includes searching for results spanning across multiple\nlines, and opt-in support for PCRE2, which provides look-around and\nbackreference support.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eAt this point, the primary reasons not to use ripgrep probably consist of one\nor more of the following:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eYou need a portable and ubiquitous tool. While ripgrep works on Windows,\nmacOS and Linux, it is not ubiquitous and it does not conform to any\nstandard such as POSIX. The best tool for this job is good old grep.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eThere still exists some other feature (or bug) not listed in this README that\nyou rely on that's in another tool that isn't in ripgrep.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eThere is a performance edge case where ripgrep doesn't do well where another\ntool does do well. (Please file a bug report!)\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eripgrep isn't possible to install on your machine or isn't available for your\nplatform. (Please file a bug report!)\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eIs it really faster than everything else?\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-is-it-really-faster-than-everything-else\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Is it really faster than everything else?\" href=\"#is-it-really-faster-than-everything-else\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eGenerally, yes. A large number of benchmarks with detailed analysis for each is\n\u003ca href=\"https://blog.burntsushi.net/ripgrep/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eavailable on my blog\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eSummarizing, ripgrep is fast because:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eIt is built on top of\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/rust-lang/regex\"\u003eRust's regex engine\u003c/a\u003e.\nRust's regex engine uses finite automata, SIMD and aggressive literal\noptimizations to make searching very fast. (PCRE2 support can be opted into\nwith the \u003ccode\u003e-P/--pcre2\u003c/code\u003e flag.)\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eRust's regex library maintains performance with full Unicode support by\nbuilding UTF-8 decoding directly into its deterministic finite automaton\nengine.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eIt supports searching with either memory maps or by searching incrementally\nwith an intermediate buffer. The former is better for single files and the\nlatter is better for large directories. ripgrep chooses the best searching\nstrategy for you automatically.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eApplies your ignore patterns in \u003ccode\u003e.gitignore\u003c/code\u003e files using a\n\u003ca href=\"https://docs.rs/regex/1/regex/struct.RegexSet.html\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eRegexSet\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e.\nThat means a single file path can be matched against multiple glob patterns\nsimultaneously.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eIt uses a lock-free parallel recursive directory iterator, courtesy of\n\u003ca href=\"https://docs.rs/crossbeam\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003ecrossbeam\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e and\n\u003ca href=\"https://docs.rs/ignore\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eignore\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" href=\"https://beyondgrep.com/feature-comparison/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003ehttps://beyondgrep.com/feature-comparison/\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eNote that ripgrep has grown a few significant new features recently that\nare not yet present in Andy's table. 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eripgrep is written in Rust, so you'll need to grab a\n\u003ca href=\"https://www.rust-lang.org/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eRust installation\u003c/a\u003e in order to compile it.\nripgrep compiles with Rust 1.96.0 (stable) or newer. In general, ripgrep tracks\nthe latest stable release of the Rust compiler.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eTo build ripgrep:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"git clone https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep\ncd ripgrep\ncargo build --release\n./target/release/rg --version\n0.1.3\"\u003e\u003cpre class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003egit clone https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep\ncd ripgrep\ncargo build --release\n./target/release/rg --version\n0.1.3\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eNOTE:\u003c/strong\u003e In the past, ripgrep supported a \u003ccode\u003esimd-accel\u003c/code\u003e Cargo feature when\nusing a Rust nightly compiler. This only benefited UTF-16 transcoding.\nSince it required unstable features, this build mode was prone to breakage.\nBecause of that, support for it has been removed. If you want SIMD\noptimizations for UTF-16 transcoding, then you'll have to petition the\n\u003ca href=\"https://github.com/hsivonen/encoding_rs\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eencoding_rs\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/a\u003e project to use stable\nAPIs.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eFinally, optional PCRE2 support can be built with ripgrep by enabling the\n\u003ccode\u003epcre2\u003c/code\u003e feature:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"cargo build --release --features 'pcre2'\"\u003e\u003cpre class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003ecargo build --release --features 'pcre2'\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eEnabling the PCRE2 feature works with a stable Rust compiler and will\nattempt to automatically find and link with your system's PCRE2 library via\n\u003ccode\u003epkg-config\u003c/code\u003e. If one doesn't exist, then ripgrep will build PCRE2 from source\nusing your system's C compiler and then statically link it into the final\nexecutable. Static linking can be forced even when there is an available PCRE2\nsystem library by either building ripgrep with the MUSL target or by setting\n\u003ccode\u003ePCRE2_SYS_STATIC=1\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eripgrep can be built with the MUSL target on Linux by first installing the MUSL\nlibrary on your system (consult your friendly neighborhood package manager).\nThen you just need to add MUSL support to your Rust toolchain and rebuild\nripgrep, which yields a fully static executable:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"rustup target add x86_64-unknown-linux-musl\ncargo build --release --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl\"\u003e\u003cpre class=\"notranslate\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003erustup target add x86_64-unknown-linux-musl\ncargo build --release --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eApplying the \u003ccode\u003e--features\u003c/code\u003e flag from above works as expected. If you want to\nbuild a static executable with MUSL and with PCRE2, then you will need to have\n\u003ccode\u003emusl-gcc\u003c/code\u003e installed, which might be in a separate package from the actual\nMUSL library, depending on your Linux distribution.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eRunning tests\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-running-tests\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Running tests\" href=\"#running-tests\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eripgrep is relatively well-tested, including both unit tests and integration\ntests. container-lg" itemprop="text"><div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">ripgrep (rg)</h2><a id="user-content-ripgrep-rg" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: ripgrep (rg)" href="#ripgrep-rg"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">ripgrep is a line-oriented search tool that recursively searches the current
directory for a regex pattern. By default, ripgrep will respect gitignore rules
and automatically skip hidden files/directories and binary files. (To disable
all automatic filtering by default, use <code>rg -uuu</code>.) ripgrep has first class
support on Windows, macOS and Linux, with binary downloads available for <a href="https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep/releases">every
release</a>. ripgrep is similar to
other popular search tools like The Silver Searcher, ack and grep.</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep/actions"><img src="https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep/workflows/ci/badge.svg" alt="Build status" style="max-width: 100%;"></a>
<a href="https://crates.io/crates/ripgrep" rel="nofollow"><img src="https://camo.githubusercontent.com/2fd0b64db5dfee9c0215772ce7c72fc7ea163209b3c0ceae9614947409bb578f/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f6372617465732f762f726970677265702e737667" alt="Crates.io" data-canonical-src="https://img.shields.io/crates/v/ripgrep.svg" style="max-width: 100%;"></a>
<a href="https://repology.org/project/ripgrep/badges" rel="nofollow"><img src="https://camo.githubusercontent.com/b46d56393c0e4c7df2831208a8272621178b9798084cd7cbba5aac5f5ef6436e/68747470733a2f2f7265706f6c6f67792e6f72672f62616467652f74696e792d7265706f732f726970677265702e737667" alt="Packaging status" data-canonical-src="https://repology.org/badge/tiny-repos/ripgrep.svg" style="max-width: 100%;"></a></p>
<p dir="auto">Dual-licensed under MIT or the <a href="https://unlicense.org" rel="nofollow">UNLICENSE</a>.</p>

<p dir="auto">Please see the <a href="/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/CHANGELOG.md">CHANGELOG</a> for a release history.</p>

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Quick examples comparing tools</h3><a id="user-content-quick-examples-comparing-tools" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Quick examples comparing tools" href="#quick-examples-comparing-tools"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">This example searches the entire
<a href="https://github.com/BurntSushi/linux">Linux kernel source tree</a>
(after running <code>make defconfig &amp;&amp; make -j8</code>) for <code>[A-Z]+_SUSPEND</code>, where
all matches must be words. Timings were collected on a system with an Intel
i9-12900K 5.2 GHz.</p>
<p dir="auto">Please remember that a single benchmark is never enough! See my
<a href="https://blog.burntsushi.net/ripgrep/" rel="nofollow">blog post on ripgrep</a>
for a very detailed comparison with more benchmarks and analysis.</p>

<p dir="auto">Here's another benchmark on the same corpus as above that disregards gitignore
files and searches with a whitelist instead. The corpus is the same as in the
previous benchmark, and the flags passed to each command ensure that they are
doing equivalent work:</p>
<markdown-accessiblity-table><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Tool</th>
<th>Command</th>
<th>Line count</th>
<th>Time</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td>ripgrep</td>
<td><code>rg -uuu -tc -n -w '[A-Z]+_SUSPEND'</code></td>
<td>447</td>
<td><strong>0.063s</strong> (1.00x)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://github.com/Genivia/ugrep">ugrep</a></td>
<td><code>ugrep -r -n --include='*.c' --include='*.h' -w '[A-Z]+_SUSPEND'</code></td>
<td>447</td>
<td>0.607s (9.62x)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://www.gnu.org/software/grep/" rel="nofollow">GNU grep</a></td>
<td><code>grep -E -r -n --include='*.c' --include='*.h' -w '[A-Z]+_SUSPEND'</code></td>
<td>447</td>
<td>0.674s (10.69x)</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>
<p dir="auto">Now we'll move to searching on single large file. Here is a straight-up
comparison between ripgrep, ugrep and GNU grep on a file cached in memory
(~13GB, <a href="https://object.pouta.csc.fi/OPUS-OpenSubtitles/v2018/mono/en.txt.gz" rel="nofollow"><code>OpenSubtitles.raw.en.gz</code></a>, decompressed):</p>
<markdown-accessiblity-table><table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Tool</th>
<th>Command</th>
<th>Line count</th>
<th>Time</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td>ripgrep (Unicode)</td>
<td><code>rg -w 'Sherlock [A-Z]\w+'</code></td>
<td>7882</td>
<td><strong>1.042s</strong> (1.00x)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://github.com/Genivia/ugrep">ugrep</a></td>
<td><code>ugrep -w 'Sherlock [A-Z]\w+'</code></td>
<td>7882</td>
<td>1.339s (1.28x)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><a href="https://www.gnu.org/software/grep/" rel="nofollow">GNU grep (Unicode)</a></td>
<td><code>LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8 egrep -w 'Sherlock [A-Z]\w+'</code></td>
<td>7882</td>
<td>6.577s (6.31x)</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>
<p dir="auto">In the above benchmark, passing the <code>-n</code> flag (for showing line numbers)
increases the times to <code>1.664s</code> for ripgrep and <code>9.484s</code> for GNU grep. ugrep
times are unaffected by the presence or absence of <code>-n</code>.</p>
<p dir="auto">Beware of performance cliffs though:</p>
<markdown-accessiblity-table><table>
<thead>
<tr>

<th>Time</th>

</tr>

<td>485</td>
<td>28.973s (27.51x)</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>
<p dir="auto">And performance can drop precipitously across the board when searching big

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Why should I use ripgrep?</h3><a id="user-content-why-should-i-use-ripgrep" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Why should I use ripgrep?" href="#why-should-i-use-ripgrep"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>It can replace many use cases served by other search tools
because it contains most of their features and is generally faster. (See
<a href="/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/FAQ.md#posix4ever">the FAQ</a> for more details on whether ripgrep can truly
replace grep.)</li>
<li>Like other tools specialized to code search, ripgrep defaults to
<a href="/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#recursive-search">recursive search</a> and does <a href="/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#automatic-filtering">automatic
filtering</a>. Namely, ripgrep won't search files
ignored by your <code>.gitignore</code>/<code>.ignore</code>/<code>.rgignore</code> files, it won't search
hidden files and it won't search binary files. Automatic filtering can be
disabled with <code>rg -uuu</code>.</li>
<li>ripgrep can <a href="/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#manual-filtering-file-types">search specific types of files</a>.
For example, <code>rg -tpy foo</code> limits your search to Python files and <code>rg -Tjs foo</code> excludes JavaScript files from your search. ripgrep can be taught about
new file types with custom matching rules.</li>
<li>ripgrep supports many features found in <code>grep</code>, such as showing the context
of search results, searching multiple patterns, highlighting matches with
color and full Unicode support. Unlike GNU grep, ripgrep stays fast while
supporting Unicode (which is always on).</li>
<li>ripgrep has optional support for switching its regex engine to use PCRE2.
Among other things, this makes it possible to use look-around and
backreferences in your patterns, which are not supported in ripgrep's default
regex engine. PCRE2 support can be enabled with <code>-P/--pcre2</code> (use PCRE2
always) or <code>--auto-hybrid-regex</code> (use PCRE2 only if needed). An alternative
syntax is provided via the <code>--engine (default|pcre2|auto)</code> option.</li>
<li>ripgrep has <a href="/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#replacements">rudimentary support for replacements</a>,
which permit rewriting output based on what was matched.</li>
<li>ripgrep supports <a href="/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#file-encoding">searching files in text encodings</a>
other than UTF-8, such as UTF-16, latin-1, GBK, EUC-JP, Shift_JIS and more.
(Some support for automatically detecting UTF-16 is provided. Other text
encodings must be specifically specified with the <code>-E/--encoding</code> flag.)</li>
<li>ripgrep supports searching files compressed in a common format (brotli,
bzip2, gzip, lz4, lzma, xz, or zstandard) with the <code>-z/--search-zip</code> flag.</li>
<li>ripgrep supports
<a href="/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#preprocessor">arbitrary input preprocessing filters</a>
which could be PDF text extraction, less supported decompression, decrypting,
automatic encoding detection and so on.</li>
<li>ripgrep can be configured via a
<a href="/BurntSushi/ripgrep/blob/master/GUIDE.md#configuration-file">configuration file</a>.</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">In other words, use ripgrep if you like speed, filtering by default, fewer
bugs and Unicode support.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Why shouldn't I use ripgrep?</h3><a id="user-content-why-shouldnt-i-use-ripgrep" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Why shouldn't I use ripgrep?" href="#why-shouldnt-i-use-ripgrep"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Despite initially not wanting to add every feature under the sun to ripgrep,
over time, ripgrep has grown support for most features found in other file
searching tools. This includes searching for results spanning across multiple
lines, and opt-in support for PCRE2, which provides look-around and
backreference support.</p>
<p dir="auto">At this point, the primary reasons not to use ripgrep probably consist of one
or more of the following:</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>You need a portable and ubiquitous tool. While ripgrep works on Windows,
macOS and Linux, it is not ubiquitous and it does not conform to any
standard such as POSIX. The best tool for this job is good old grep.</li>
<li>There still exists some other feature (or bug) not listed in this README that
you rely on that's in another tool that isn't in ripgrep.</li>
<li>There is a performance edge case where ripgrep doesn't do well where another
tool does do well. (Please file a bug report!)</li>
<li>ripgrep isn't possible to install on your machine or isn't available for your
platform. (Please file a bug report!)</li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Is it really faster than everything else?</h3><a id="user-content-is-it-really-faster-than-everything-else" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Is it really faster than everything else?" href="#is-it-really-faster-than-everything-else"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Generally, yes. A large number of benchmarks with detailed analysis for each is
<a href="https://blog.burntsushi.net/ripgrep/" rel="nofollow">available on my blog</a>.</p>
<p dir="auto">Summarizing, ripgrep is fast because:</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>It is built on top of
<a href="https://github.com/rust-lang/regex">Rust's regex engine</a>.
Rust's regex engine uses finite automata, SIMD and aggressive literal
optimizations to make searching very fast. (PCRE2 support can be opted into
with the <code>-P/--pcre2</code> flag.)</li>
<li>Rust's regex library maintains performance with full Unicode support by
building UTF-8 decoding directly into its deterministic finite automaton
engine.</li>
<li>It supports searching with either memory maps or by searching incrementally
with an intermediate buffer. The former is better for single files and the
latter is better for large directories. ripgrep chooses the best searching
strategy for you automatically.</li>
<li>Applies your ignore patterns in <code>.gitignore</code> files using a
<a href="https://docs.rs/regex/1/regex/struct.RegexSet.html" rel="nofollow"><code>RegexSet</code></a>.
That means a single file path can be matched against multiple glob patterns
simultaneously.</li>
<li>It uses a lock-free parallel recursive directory iterator, courtesy of
<a href="https://docs.rs/crossbeam" rel="nofollow"><code>crossbeam</code></a> and
<a href="https://docs.rs/ignore" rel="nofollow"><code>ignore</code></a>.</li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Feature comparison</h3><a id="user-content-feature-comparison" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Feature comparison" href="#feature-comparison"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Andy Lester, author of <a href="https://beyondgrep.com/" rel="nofollow">ack</a>, has published an
excellent table comparing the features of ack, ag, git-grep, GNU grep and
ripgrep: <a href="https://beyondgrep.com/feature-comparison/" rel="nofollow">https://beyondgrep.com/feature-comparison/</a></p>
<p dir="auto">Note that ripgrep has grown a few significant new features recently that
are not yet present in Andy's table. This includes, but is not limited to,
configuration files, passthru, support for searching compressed files,
multiline search and opt-in fancy regex support via PCRE2.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Playground</h3><a id="user-content-playground" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Playground" href="#playground"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">If you'd like to try ripgrep before installing, there's an unofficial
<a href="https://codapi.org/ripgrep/" rel="nofollow">playground</a> and an <a href="https://codapi.org/try/ripgrep/" rel="nofollow">interactive
tutorial</a>.</p>
<p dir="auto">If you have any questions about these, please open an issue in the <a href="https://github.com/nalgeon/tryxinyminutes">tutorial
repo</a>.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Installation</h3><a id="user-content-installation" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Installation" href="#installation"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">The binary name for ripgrep is <code>rg</code>.</p>
<p dir="auto"><strong><a href="https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep/releases">Archives of precompiled binaries for ripgrep are available for Windows,
macOS and Linux.</a></strong> Linux and
Windows binaries are static executables. Users of platforms not explicitly
mentioned below are advised to download one of these archives.</p>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>macOS Homebrew</strong> or a <strong>Linuxbrew</strong> user, then you can install
ripgrep from homebrew-core:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="brew install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>brew install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>MacPorts</strong> user, then you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://www.macports.org/ports.php?by=name&amp;substr=ripgrep" rel="nofollow">official ports</a>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo port install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo port install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Windows Chocolatey</strong> user, then you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://chocolatey.org/packages/ripgrep" rel="nofollow">official repo</a>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="choco install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>choco install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Windows Scoop</strong> user, then you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://github.com/ScoopInstaller/Main/blob/master/bucket/ripgrep.json">official bucket</a>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="scoop install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>scoop install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Windows Winget</strong> user, then you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://github.com/microsoft/winget-pkgs/tree/master/manifests/b/BurntSushi/ripgrep">winget-pkgs</a>
repository:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="winget install BurntSushi.ripgrep.MSVC"><pre class="notranslate"><code>winget install BurntSushi.ripgrep.MSVC
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're an <strong>Arch Linux</strong> user, then you can install ripgrep from the official repos:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo pacman -S ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo pacman -S ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Gentoo</strong> user, you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://packages.gentoo.org/packages/sys-apps/ripgrep" rel="nofollow">official repo</a>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo emerge sys-apps/ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo emerge sys-apps/ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Fedora</strong> user, you can install ripgrep from official
repositories.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo dnf install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo dnf install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're an <strong>openSUSE</strong> user, ripgrep is included in <strong>openSUSE Tumbleweed</strong>
and <strong>openSUSE Leap</strong> since 15.1.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo zypper install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo zypper install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>CentOS Stream 10</strong> user, you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://docs.fedoraproject.org/en-US/epel/getting-started/" rel="nofollow">EPEL</a> repository:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo dnf config-manager --set-enabled crb
sudo dnf install https://dl.fedoraproject.org/pub/epel/epel-release-latest-10.noarch.rpm
sudo dnf install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo dnf config-manager --set-enabled crb
sudo dnf install https://dl.fedoraproject.org/pub/epel/epel-release-latest-10.noarch.rpm
sudo dnf install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Red Hat 10</strong> user, you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://docs.fedoraproject.org/en-US/epel/getting-started/" rel="nofollow">EPEL</a> repository:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo subscription-manager repos --enable codeready-builder-for-rhel-10-$(arch)-rpms
sudo dnf install https://dl.fedoraproject.org/pub/epel/epel-release-latest-10.noarch.rpm
sudo dnf install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo subscription-manager repos --enable codeready-builder-for-rhel-10-$(arch)-rpms
sudo dnf install https://dl.fedoraproject.org/pub/epel/epel-release-latest-10.noarch.rpm
sudo dnf install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Rocky Linux 10</strong> user, you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://docs.fedoraproject.org/en-US/epel/getting-started/" rel="nofollow">EPEL</a> repository:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo dnf install https://dl.fedoraproject.org/pub/epel/epel-release-latest-10.noarch.rpm
sudo dnf install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo dnf install https://dl.fedoraproject.org/pub/epel/epel-release-latest-10.noarch.rpm
sudo dnf install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Nix</strong> user, you can install ripgrep from
<a href="https://github.com/NixOS/nixpkgs/blob/master/pkgs/by-name/ri/ripgrep/package.nix">nixpkgs</a>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="nix-env --install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>nix-env --install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Flox</strong> user, you can install ripgrep as follows:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="flox install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>flox install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Guix</strong> user, you can install ripgrep from the official
package collection:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="guix install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>guix install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Debian</strong> user (or a user of a Debian derivative like <strong>Ubuntu</strong>),
then ripgrep can be installed using a binary <code>.deb</code> file provided in each
<a href="https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep/releases">ripgrep release</a>.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="curl -LO https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep/releases/download/14.1.1/ripgrep_14.1.1-1_amd64.deb
sudo dpkg -i ripgrep_14.1.1-1_amd64.deb"><pre class="notranslate"><code>curl -LO https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep/releases/download/14.1.1/ripgrep_14.1.1-1_amd64.deb
sudo dpkg -i ripgrep_14.1.1-1_amd64.deb
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you run Debian stable, ripgrep is <a href="https://tracker.debian.org/pkg/rust-ripgrep" rel="nofollow">officially maintained by
Debian</a>, although its version may
be older than the <code>deb</code> package available in the previous step.</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo apt-get install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo apt-get install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're an <strong>Ubuntu Cosmic (18.10)</strong> (or newer) user, ripgrep is
<a href="https://launchpad.net/ubuntu/+source/rust-ripgrep" rel="nofollow">available</a> using the same
packaging as Debian:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo apt-get install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo apt-get install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">(N.B. Various snaps for ripgrep on Ubuntu are also available, but none of them
seem to work right and generate a number of very strange bug reports that I
don't know how to fix and don't have the time to fix. Therefore, it is no
longer a recommended installation option.)</p>
<p dir="auto">If you're an <strong>ALT</strong> user, you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://packages.altlinux.org/en/search?name=ripgrep" rel="nofollow">official repo</a>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo apt-get install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo apt-get install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>FreeBSD</strong> user, then you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://www.freshports.org/textproc/ripgrep/" rel="nofollow">official ports</a>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo pkg install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo pkg install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're an <strong>OpenBSD</strong> user, then you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://openports.se/textproc/ripgrep" rel="nofollow">official ports</a>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="doas pkg_add ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>doas pkg_add ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>NetBSD</strong> user, then you can install ripgrep from
<a href="https://pkgsrc.se/textproc/ripgrep" rel="nofollow">pkgsrc</a>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo pkgin install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo pkgin install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Haiku x86_64</strong> user, then you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://github.com/haikuports/haikuports/tree/master/sys-apps/ripgrep">official ports</a>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo pkgman install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo pkgman install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Haiku x86_gcc2</strong> user, then you can install ripgrep from the
same port as Haiku x86_64 using the x86 secondary architecture build:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo pkgman install ripgrep_x86"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo pkgman install ripgrep_x86
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Void Linux</strong> user, then you can install ripgrep from the
<a href="https://voidlinux.org/packages/?arch=x86_64&amp;q=ripgrep" rel="nofollow">official repository</a>:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="sudo xbps-install -Syv ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>sudo xbps-install -Syv ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">If you're a <strong>Rust programmer</strong>, ripgrep can be installed with <code>cargo</code>.</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>Note that the minimum supported version of Rust for ripgrep is <strong>1.96.0</strong>,
although ripgrep may work with older versions.</li>
<li>Note that the binary may be bigger than expected because it contains debug
symbols. This is intentional. To remove debug symbols and therefore reduce
the file size, run <code>strip</code> on the binary.</li>
</ul>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="cargo install ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>cargo install ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Alternatively, one can use <a href="https://github.com/cargo-bins/cargo-binstall"><code>cargo binstall</code></a> to install a ripgrep
binary directly from GitHub:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="cargo binstall ripgrep"><pre class="notranslate"><code>cargo binstall ripgrep
</code></pre></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Building</h3><a id="user-content-building" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Building" href="#building"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">ripgrep is written in Rust, so you'll need to grab a
<a href="https://www.rust-lang.org/" rel="nofollow">Rust installation</a> in order to compile it.
ripgrep compiles with Rust 1.96.0 (stable) or newer. In general, ripgrep tracks
the latest stable release of the Rust compiler.</p>
<p dir="auto">To build ripgrep:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="git clone https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep
cd ripgrep
cargo build --release
./target/release/rg --version
0.1.3"><pre class="notranslate"><code>git clone https://github.com/BurntSushi/ripgrep
cd ripgrep
cargo build --release
./target/release/rg --version
0.1.3
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto"><strong>NOTE:</strong> In the past, ripgrep supported a <code>simd-accel</code> Cargo feature when
using a Rust nightly compiler. This only benefited UTF-16 transcoding.
Since it required unstable features, this build mode was prone to breakage.
Because of that, support for it has been removed. If you want SIMD
optimizations for UTF-16 transcoding, then you'll have to petition the
<a href="https://github.com/hsivonen/encoding_rs"><code>encoding_rs</code></a> project to use stable
APIs.</p>
<p dir="auto">Finally, optional PCRE2 support can be built with ripgrep by enabling the
<code>pcre2</code> feature:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="cargo build --release --features 'pcre2'"><pre class="notranslate"><code>cargo build --release --features 'pcre2'
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Enabling the PCRE2 feature works with a stable Rust compiler and will
attempt to automatically find and link with your system's PCRE2 library via
<code>pkg-config</code>. If one doesn't exist, then ripgrep will build PCRE2 from source
using your system's C compiler and then statically link it into the final
executable. Static linking can be forced even when there is an available PCRE2
system library by either building ripgrep with the MUSL target or by setting
<code>PCRE2_SYS_STATIC=1</code>.</p>
<p dir="auto">ripgrep can be built with the MUSL target on Linux by first installing the MUSL
library on your system (consult your friendly neighborhood package manager).
Then you just need to add MUSL support to your Rust toolchain and rebuild
ripgrep, which yields a fully static executable:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="rustup target add x86_64-unknown-linux-musl
cargo build --release --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl"><pre class="notranslate"><code>rustup target add x86_64-unknown-linux-musl
cargo build --release --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Applying the <code>--features</code> flag from above works as expected. If you want to
build a static executable with MUSL and with PCRE2, then you will need to have
<code>musl-gcc</code> installed, which might be in a separate package from the actual
MUSL library, depending on your Linux distribution.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Running tests</h3><a id="user-content-running-tests" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Running tests" href="#running-tests"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">ripgrep is relatively well-tested, including both unit tests and integration
tests. To run the full test suite, use:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="cargo test --all"><pre class="notranslate"><code>cargo test --all
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">from the repository root.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Related tools</h3><a id="user-content-related-tools" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Related tools" href="#related-tools"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><a href="https://github.com/dandavison/delta">delta</a> is a syntax highlighting
pager that supports the <code>rg --json</code> output format. So all you need to do to
make it work is <code>rg --json pattern | delta</code>. See <a href="https://dandavison.github.io/delta/grep.html" rel="nofollow">delta's manual section on
grep</a> for more details.</li>
</ul>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Vulnerability reporting</h3><a id="user-content-vulnerability-reporting" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Vulnerability reporting" href="#vulnerability-reporting"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">For reporting a security vulnerability, please
<a href="https://blog.burntsushi.net/about/" rel="nofollow">contact Andrew Gallant</a>.
The contact page has my email address and PGP public key if you wish to send an
encrypted message.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Translations</h3><a id="user-content-translations" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Translations" href="#translations"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
